Final CUA viewport overrides stopped matching the browser's CSS viewport, so responsive QA uses a source-identical isolated fixed-width iframe fixture instead of claiming native viewport qualification. `qa-clone-hashes.json` records every original preview-file hash; final cloned authority view/CSS match source. The wrapper gives the child an actual 320, 768 or 1440 CSS-pixel width and 900 height; `browser-qa.json` verifies those dimensions, both themes and scroll position. Only the isolated fixture origin permits same-origin framing; production preview source/CSP stays unchanged, with no native bridge or provider connectivity. The wrapper's outer scale 0.45 allows complete viewport overview captures; it does not change the child breakpoint width. These are browser fixture checks, not native-window, physical-touch or three-OS qualification.

At 1440 light, workbench top is 273.79px versus 1969.41px before, and visible text is 1,963 versus 9,485 characters, a 79.3% reduction. Card height is 196.90px versus 270px. All six cases show no document horizontal overflow, LNSAT first without automatic selection, five neutral unavailable segments, five initially closed disclosures and 44px controls/summaries (fractional browser measurements round from 43.9998px). Complete captures are `module-{light,dark}-{320,768,1440}.jpg`; earlier incorrectly suffixed PNG captures contain JPEG bytes and are retained as clipped diagnostic evidence, not final responsive proof.

Final review found one P3 evidence gap: the original retained-result fixture did not prove a dropped actual Tauri source waiter. The supplemental fixture drops the real blocking SelectionCompletion waiter and uses a channel/drop sentinel to prove that drain remains nonquiescent until its retained completion is discarded. The reviewer passed this repair. This is native source custody evidence, not a modal GUI, authenticated IPC delivery or complete renderer purge result.

During fixture development, an initial command omitted the desktop manifest and therefore selected a package without the encrypted feature. The first correctly targeted run had three fixture failures: an invalid ID prevented the intended poison path, and the fake vault represented an absent record as an empty present record. Corrected fixtures use a valid analyzed ID and an explicit absent record; a fresh backend proves the read-panic path independently of earlier mutex poisoning. Existing assertions were preserved. A subsequent full run passed the test modes but failed warnings-denied Clippy on a cloned reference slice in the supplemental fixture; replacing it with `std::slice::from_ref` resolved the lint without suppression. The final seven-command run below passed. These fixture corrections do not establish the cause of the earlier intermittent InputUnavailable failure.

Experience: flat remains default and constructs no scene until explicitly chosen. The alternate view uses fixed yaw/pitch/zoom with named 44px 2D editor, Overview and Focus selection controls. Focus frames an exact selected node and its direct neighbors, or an exact edge route and endpoints; selecting another record alone never moves the camera. Framing is instant, with no orbit/zoom controls, camera gestures/shortcuts, interpolation or idle loop. The depth header separates its title from the wrapping action bar. Existing bounded, user-started design Preview remains available; it is not traffic or execution.

Frames are local presentation data with finite bounds and exact admitted IDs. Resize/theme retain the focused section. Layout-only changes recompute that same focus target independently of current selection; node identity/kind or edge changes restore Overview. Every record stays in the projection and complete Outline/inspector. Empty/unsafe/oversized graphs, narrow/forced-color modes and graphics loss retain honest flat fallbacks. Focus is disabled for unavailable projections or pending native operations; 2D return remains available. Returning to a selected connection reveals its existing disclosure before focusing the exact item. Pending fields, connection gestures, saved viewport and controller history remain intact.

Review repair: the first implementation used a geometry fingerprint as topology identity, so moving a node reset focus. A separate topology fingerprint now covers node IDs/kinds and directed edges; the geometry fingerprint still drives GPU geometry updates. Tests focus node 0, select node 1, move node 0 in 2D, return to the same focused section, undo the move and remove the target. Framing causes no controller/viewport mutation. Independent review passed this correction.

Rendered QA also caught an inherited stationary flat-header click failing to select after existing pointer capture retargeted it to the node container. The binder now resolves that container through the same selection/control-connect path as the labelled node button. Source checks cover exact selection without graph/history edits, valid control connection and refused self-loop. Actual fresh-browser Check selection, then Fit and Input selection passed after repair. Existing port and drag controllers are unchanged.

Developer guidance reuses W1's six closed operation kinds/three port types, R4a compiler identities and I1 offline provider-fixture inspection. The R9 section documents current versus proposed support, declarative descriptor requirements, failure handling, isolation and an exact future offline reference/conformance packet. Dense rendering falls back to complete flat records rather than rejecting a valid authored graph. Native structural qualification remains distinct from drafts and UI compatibility. No new I1 wire field, SDK package, plugin loader, provider adapter or executable worker was added.

## Completed source foundation: R1a

Implemented a platform-neutral Rust domain and source-analysis foundation with a stdin-only CLI. It accepts bounded UTF-8 instruction bytes, preserves original byte hashes and exact line spans, detects Markdown sections deterministically, and represents extracted sections as unreviewed proposals. The caller supplies every byte; a display name never opens a path. No recursive scan, archives, network, models, hooks, scripts, execution authority, persistence or installation. The experimental contract is recorded in [source-analysis.md](source-analysis.md).

This is a bounded early implementation of R1 contracts and the pure-analysis prerequisite for R2/R3, not completion of those packets. Three-OS source-test CI now passes. Full desktop qualification, shared UI evolution, license selection and real installers remain R1/R6 follow-ups.

Controller owns architecture, contracts, integration, validation and git state. Producer roles: primary Sol controller for domain/CLI/contracts/CI and integration; native OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra high for the bounded analyzer and its tests. A fresh OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra xhigh reviewer returned PASS for the 25-file R1a diff, with no actionable P1/P2/P3 findings, then independently passed the test-fixture correction. Hosted CI closed the minimum-toolchain and three-OS source-check gap. GLM was not retried after the recorded transport failures in the preview packet; an adequate native review lane is available.

## Desktop selected-file continuation: R1b

The latest user direction is captured in [product-experience.md](product-experience.md): make the image workflows useful through complete, traceable actions. All eight originals were visually reviewed again. R1b implements one such action in a separate Tauri 2 desktop workspace: native one-file picker, bounded host read, existing deterministic parser, and an actual source/section/inspector UI with dark/light themes. [desktop-spike.md](desktop-spike.md) records commands and boundaries. The synthetic preview remains separate; its Import page links to the real analysis page, which explains when a native bridge is unavailable.

No renderer path or bytes are accepted by the bridge. The host checks file type before opening and on the opened handle, limits reads, and returns fixed non-echoing errors. The UI retains prior analysis on cancellation/rejection, ignores stale results after Clear, restores focus, and renders source as escaped text. Reports remain in memory. Persistent workspaces, semantic skills, real merge/split, harness compilation, connector actions and execution remain future work.

Producer: primary Sol controller for architecture, host/native boundary, integration, CI and evidence; OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra high for the five-file UI/controller/test slice; OpenAI GPT-5.6-Luna medium for the README. Independent OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra xhigh UI review closed a theme-button accessibility finding. Independent native-boundary review required Windows pre-open and opened-handle reparse tests; both corrections received independent PASS. The first hosted Windows core job then passed the actual reparse tests. GLM was skipped after the earlier recorded transport failures; no private payload was transferred.

## Durable local source snapshots: R2a

The current continuation adds one bounded durable loop: select a Markdown file, inspect it, explicitly Save locally, quit, reopen, list the saved record, and open it again. Native commands use an opaque `sourceId`; the renderer cannot choose a filesystem path or source body. The desktop stores source bytes and metadata in `app_local_data_dir()/source-workspace/workspace.sqlite3` only after Save. Storage is unencrypted on this computer, with owner-only Unix permissions and inherited per-user Windows ACLs; it is not an encrypted vault or tamper-proof evidence store.

Identical basename and bytes return the existing record. Changed bytes or basename create a separate immutable snapshot. Reopen reanalyzes persisted bytes with the deterministic analyzer and preserves `authority: none` plus `unreviewed` fragments. Save, list, open and clear preserve the specified current-report and failure semantics; malformed identity/schema/database records fail closed rather than being silently omitted. Limits are 128 snapshots, 256 KiB per source and 64 MiB database. No automatic save, browser source storage, upload, provider call, delete or retention policy exists.

## Reviewed capability revisions: R2b

The R2b continuation adds a local Skills workspace derived from an explicitly saved source section. Capability creation copies the section's exact original bytes and span into an immutable provenance record. The first revision is a draft; later edits create immutable revisions with parent links, exact-content SHA-256 identities and retained history. A local operator can inspect and mark the current revision reviewed, while edits to a reviewed revision create an unreviewed successor. Source reports and fragments remain unchanged, and every capability result retains `authority: none`. Local content review does not establish semantic preservation, compatibility, authenticated authorship, publication, execution permission or any other authority.

The native desktop owns five typed commands: list, create, open, revise and review. The renderer supplies only bounded titles, content and opaque IDs; it cannot supply a path or original bytes. The preview adds a Skills workbench with library, draft editing, historical read-only comparison, provenance, conflict retention and explicit review gating. Native capability commands are desktop-only; the CLI has no alternate capability path. The exact implementation ledger is [docs/changed-files.txt](changed-files.txt), including the domain, store, native and preview files in this packet.

R2b uses lazy schema 2 migration. Existing schema 1 stores remain readable, and source-only Save plus read/list operations do not migrate or create capability tables. The first explicit capability creation upgrades the validated database in the same immediate transaction that writes the capability; DDL, version update and first capability commit are atomic. Interrupted or failed migration rolls back to schema 1 with existing source intact. Schema 2 adds `capabilities`, `revisions` and `reviews` while preserving the original snapshots table byte for byte. The limits are 128 capabilities, 32 revisions per capability, 1,024 total revisions, 160 UTF-8 bytes for a nonempty single-line title, 256 KiB UTF-8 content without NUL and the existing 64 MiB database ceiling. There is no automatic eviction, backup/restore or downgrade path. An older Rangoon binary rejects schema 2 rather than overwriting it. SQLite remains unencrypted application-local data.

Producer ownership is explicit: the primary Sol controller owns architecture, domain/store/native integration, validation and release judgment; OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra high owns the bounded UI and tests; OpenAI GPT-5.6-Luna medium owns documentation synchronization. Fresh OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra xhigh storage and UI code reviews passed after fixes. A1 remains merged in PR #4 at `adbaff16035048275fcbb6668aae92606c330473`. V1 source `eec2a19bfd7d362ee56a2c3038a44aada3a44988` remains PR #5 with hosted runner-acquisition cancellations recorded as infrastructure cancellations, not code failures. R2b was developed on `codex/reviewed-capability-revisions`; its Git commit and review PR identify the published source. This section records the local evidence before main publication.

Local validation: `cargo test --workspace --locked --offline` passed 51 tests, including 21 store tests; three ignored subprocess helpers were explicitly invoked and killed by their recovery parents. Native tests passed 5 tests. Core/native formatting and warnings-denied Clippy passed. `npm run check`, `npm run validate:visuals`, `npm test` (34 tests, including 12 Skills tests), and `node scripts/validate-review.mjs` passed. The preview-server test required an authorized loopback-capable run after the sandbox rejected binding to `127.0.0.1` with `EPERM`; that infrastructure error was not hidden or counted as a passing run. Recovery coverage includes interrupted schema migration, a forced `SQLITE_FULL` successor write, and a killed schema 2 transaction containing a successor, review and head update. These are transaction-recovery tests, not production crash qualification.

Through the native picker and real IPC, the source was saved, the Review section (bytes 28–87, lines 3–5) became a skill named `Review changes`, its exact 59-byte first revision was reviewed, and a 101-byte edit became an unreviewed successor. Historical view showed the original 59-byte reviewed revision with mutation controls disabled. Returning to current restored the 101-byte successor, which was explicitly reviewed. The process was quit, confirmed absent, relaunched, and the library reopened one skill with both revisions and review states intact. Authority remained none; original source identity was `source:2c0a616d8d080a745118c5ccbae7217fdfd724cfc8a0b8bb57053eeec69301b8`. No browser bridge was injected. ## Workspace data controls: R2c

Published source: `main` at `538f319011b0611665de27ad32d6c528a8310d98`, from PR #7 reviewed head `32a5b446e0baedb04c07931636be01d47df828bd`. The [R2c specification](workspace-data-controls.md) defines portable backup, additive restore and dependency-aware logical deletion through a real Workspace page. Sources, skill histories and local review annotations remain inert. The archive and SQLite workspace are currently unencrypted. Crypto protection and authenticated history remain a separate proposal; ordinary SHA-256 checksums are not authentication.

The format is typed application data, not a raw SQLite database import. Native pickers own paths, export creates a new destination exclusively, and restore validates the archive against application-owned tables before any destination write. Existing skills are kept unchanged. Restore/deletion confirmations bind to an opaque logical-state digest; stale state fails before mutation and is never automatically retried. Source deletion lists and blocks dependent skills; skill deletion removes only its own history and local reviews. Original files are never modified. A backup's historical review records are unauthenticated and never become current approval or execution authority.

Design review identified and corrected SQLite page-overhead capacity accounting, stale retry ambiguity, underspecified manifest fields and canonical page-size mismatch. The encoder/decoder now reconstruct canonical 4 KiB-page SQLite under the 64 MiB allocation cap; newly initialized files match it, and noncanonical empty files are rejected rather than reset. A verified empty file left by a failed first restore can be previewed and explicitly initialized without silent replacement.

Local source validation passed: core formatting, 63 Rust workspace tests (including 31 store tests), warnings-denied core Clippy, seven native tests, native formatting, warnings-denied native Clippy, syntax, visual-asset, repository-review and diff-whitespace checks. The preview-server test first hit the sandbox's loopback `EPERM`; the authorized loopback-capable rerun passed the full 47 JavaScript tests. Three ignored subprocess helpers are invoked by their recovery parents. Store tests include independent archive bytes, additive preservation, dependency blocking, stale confirmation, canonical allocation overhead, full-database rollback and interrupted-store recovery. These are source tests, not proof against malicious local rewriting.

Fresh native OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra xhigh contract and UI reviews closed their actionable findings. The primary controller owns integration; the Terra high worker owns the bounded Workspace view/controller tests. UI fixes retain a usable restore preview after picker cancellation, keep controls busy through library refresh, preserve verified mutation receipts when refresh fails, and omit removal counts when a dependent source cannot be deleted. The browser-only 320-pixel Workspace state remains explicitly unavailable; see [narrow browser capture](screenshots/r2c-workspace-browser-320.jpg).

Native export produced a 1,441-byte backup with SHA-256 `8ac44a7c9f9ed6ae071cac22ae7b37067817253b2d9fd9b46c312712269d4078`. ### R3 composition recovery: local implementation

The [recovery refinement](composition-recovery.md) defines canonical V2 archives containing source bytes, ordinary and composed histories, reviews, recipes, applications and exact live derivation membership. The new Rust APIs export validated records, decode bounded V1/V2 archives, plan additive restore, recheck complete confirmations inside an immediate transaction, and remove explicitly selected records only when surviving dependents permit it. Restore keeps existing histories and local metadata, requires the final pinned-input closure, and never recreates absent outputs from an application's target list. Schema migration and restore writes commit together; reads and no-addition restores do not promote schemas.

The primary controller implemented the storage, format and recovery code. An OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra high worker independently generated Python `hashlib`/`struct` vectors for an empty archive and a partially retained composition; complete expected bytes, trailer hashes and archive identities match Rust. Native GPT review is used; no source transfer to GLM was made. No native command or UI currently calls the new APIs. The old V1 native recovery APIs retain their behavior and reject schema 3. A composition apply writer, durable drafts and the complete native workbench remain incomplete.

Fourteen targeted recovery tests passed locally, including exact round trips, independent bytes, newer local history, missing pinned inputs, self/external dependencies, partial output membership, local timestamp/review preservation, stale confirmations, malformed descriptors and recomputed-trailer payload rejection. A valid small canonical archive is accepted; a 64 MiB raw-content archive is rejected for SQLite allocation overhead. Disposable subprocess tests interrupt the actual public restore after migration and row insertion, interrupt public deletion after row removal, and force page-allocation failure before and after migration. Reopening preserves prior schema, rows, reviews and heads, and a subsequent valid operation succeeds. The fault controls compile only into tests. One ignored subprocess helper is invoked by its parent tests.

Initial fault-test setup used an overlong synthetic line and was corrected to bounded lines; no storage defect was involved. The full workspace passed 114 tests, including 55 store tests and four ignored subprocess helpers invoked by their parents. The initial sandbox run failed only at the existing disposable Unix-socket fixture (`Operation not permitted`); the authorized rerun passed. Native seven tests and `cargo check`, formatting, warnings-denied workspace Clippy, repository-review validation (130 authored text files and eight reference image hashes), and diff-whitespace checks passed. Fresh independent OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra xhigh source, test and documentation review passed with no actionable P1/P2/P3 findings; the reviewer did not rerun the controller's tests. These are pre-publication receipts; exact-commit CI and main publication remain pending. Archives and SQLite remain plaintext with unkeyed consistency hashes; these tests establish recovery behavior, not malicious-tampering resistance, encryption, authenticated approval, released OS support or security certification.

### R3 atomic composition saves: local implementation

The primary controller implemented `apply_composition` against a retained, non-deserializable Rust preview with a private normalized request. Preview checks actual saved inputs, destination heads, resource limits and the proposed complete record state. Commit checks explicit acknowledgment, current workspace state and exact recalculated preview inside an immediate transaction before any migration or row write. Every new/append/mixed output commits together with its recipe, application and derivation. Actual v1 capability details return in output order. Prior source bytes, birth origins, revisions and reviews remain intact; each new revision is unreviewed with no execution authority.

Versioned source save and ordinary create/revise/review APIs support both source-born and composition-born skills after schema 3 migration. Ordinary successors retain birth while explicitly reporting ordinary provenance. The original APIs still reject schema 3. Native commands, renderer validators and UI have not switched to these APIs, so the native workbench is not yet a composition workflow. Native opaque preview handles, durable drafts and all-three-OS GUI qualification remain required.

Targeted source tests cover mixed commits and backup round trips, missing acknowledgment, forged preview data, stale confirmation and no automatic retry, blocked editable drafts, ordinary edits/reviews after composition, pinned historical inputs, actual merges into existing derived skills, intentional recreation after explicit deletion, per-skill capacity and concurrent single-winner commits. Disposable child processes interrupt the real save API after migration or the first output, including migration from schema 1, and force allocation failure before/after schema 2 migration. Prior canonical backups, schema versions and reviewed heads survive; subsequent explicit saves succeed. Fault controls compile out of production. A source-save regression rejects corrupt existing history before adding any source.

The other 59 workspace tests passed in the earlier full run; together these checks cover 126 workspace tests. ### R3 native composition and v1 surface integration: local implementation

The [native composition contract](composition-native.md) connects the existing atomic store to two bundled-window commands using bounded raw UTF-8 JSON. The host issues a 32-byte operating-system-random preview handle and retains the normalized request, exact materialization and expected state. It marks the handle attempted before accessing storage, rejects reuse after any attempted commit, and consumes the matching slot after a verified commit. Missing, replaced, previous-process, stale and unacknowledged confirmations cannot write. Random-source failure never falls back to a timestamp or counter. A new preview and acknowledgment are required after an uncertain result.

Existing native source saving, Skills and Workspace move to composition-aware APIs together. Source and composition birth origins remain distinct from revision provenance. Source-origin Skills retains original text; composed Skills never invents a source section. Ordinary edits retain birth while reporting ordinary revision provenance. Workspace inventory exposes actual recovery counts; V2 export and V1/V2 additive restore preserve composition records. Native restore and deletion retain the entire issued plan and compare confirmation selections before the store revalidates it. This remains plaintext local data with unkeyed content identities and `authority: none`.

The primary controller owns native commands, retained sessions, integration, tests and documentation. Two native OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra high workers updated the disjoint Skills and Workspace renderer/model/test files. Fresh OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra xhigh reviews initially found a reusable failed-commit handle, semantically impossible renderer origins, a false-positive history test and metric-grid separators still assuming five tiles. The controller and workers addressed these findings. Fresh independent source/contract and rendered UI re-reviews passed with no actionable P1/P2/P3 findings. The source reviewer inspected the final diff and documentation without rerunning the controller tests; the UI reviewer inspected rendered fixtures and the stylesheet. Exact-commit CI and publication remain pending. No GLM invocation or external source transfer was used.

Local validation passed 52 JavaScript tests and 15 native tests, a native executable build without installers, native formatting and warnings-denied native Clippy, JavaScript syntax, the existing 26-icon/four-size visual checks and asset hashes, repository-review validation, and diff-whitespace checks. The first JavaScript run failed only because the sandbox denied the loopback server fixture (`listen EPERM`); the authorized rerun passed. New native coverage includes closed raw payload decoding, random-source failure, handle replacement/consumption, stale saves, rejected retry after unchanged-state failure and simulated lost commit confirmation, exact retained deletion/restore plans, and a schema 3 save/edit/review/source-save/export/restore/delete loop in disposable stores. These helper tests do not prove the native WebView's raw IPC transport.

Synthetic rendered QA uses a fresh headless Chrome profile with reduced motion, actual renderer modules and explicit fixture labels. The twelve Skills/Workspace combinations at 320, 768 and 1440 pixels in dark/light themes have no document overflow, unnamed buttons or duplicate IDs. The eight Workspace metrics use four columns on wide screens and two on tablet/narrow screens, with grid separators on every row. The [measurement receipt](screenshots/r3-v1-layout-measurements.json), [wide dark Workspace](screenshots/r3-v1-workspace-fixture-dark-1440.jpg), [tablet dark Workspace](screenshots/r3-v1-workspace-fixture-dark-768.jpg), and [narrow light Skills](screenshots/r3-v1-skills-fixture-light-320.jpg) are layout evidence, not saved user data or native GUI proof. ### R4a instruction compiler: local implementation and validation

The local `rangoon-compile` crate implements the reviewed pure R4a contract for the two frozen profiles `agents_md_v1` and `claude_md_v1`. ### Native Compile inspection: published source and qualification boundary

The [native inspection contract](compilation-ui.md) adds a real Compile route over exact saved capability/revision IDs and the two fixed profiles. The closed raw JSON command is bounded to 4,096 bytes before decoding, shares the native operation guard, and returns the validated report plus host-serialized canonical candidate manifest. It does not accept source content, review assertions, paths or renderer manifests. It does not write records, export files or change the source-analysis session.

The workbench separates selection, generated text, diagnostics and evidence. Historical revisions remain selectable; current-head observation is separate. Known workspace writes invalidate retained reports, explicit refresh resolves the same revision, and a fresh Compile action replaces stale evidence. Navigation to Skills preserves existing unsaved drafts. The controller compares report selection, profile, exact text and diagnostics with the opened record, checks manifest bytes/hash, and recomputes compilation and candidate identities. These are consistency checks, not authentication; native GUI and target-loader qualification remain separate.

### Portable instruction bundles: local implementation and review

The accepted V1 lifecycle and [bundle contract](instruction-bundle.md) now have a pure exact-byte encoder/verifier in `rangoon-compile` and bounded selected-file helpers in `rangoon-host`. The format binds canonical candidate-manifest bytes and exact artifact bytes under explicit lengths and a checksum of all preceding bytes. Inspection also returns a whole-file digest and a framed bundle identity. The 295,016-byte total limit is checked before parsing; private closed wire records, canonical reconstruction, profile/identity/review/diagnostic validation and exact artifact digest checks reject inconsistent input. Public compiler outputs remain serializable only. Existing frozen compiler vectors are unchanged.

This establishes internal consistency only, with `authority: none`. A coherently rewritten file can pass verification. It is not authenticated review, complete source-graph validation, encryption, rollback protection, target compatibility or execution authorization. Native Export/Inspect commands, UI and GUI qualification are not implemented by this packet; the native integration section of the contract describes the next work.

Selected-file helpers require an absolute path and portable `.rangoon-instructions` basename, bound reads through a regular opened handle, verify before exclusive creation, never overwrite, and use Unix owner-only creation permissions. Windows path tests distinguish disk/UNC and their explicit verbatim forms from device/arbitrary namespaces. Parent directories are not held or confined. Write/sync failure can leave a partial or complete file; the helper does not silently remove or retry it. Tests cover concurrent creators, malformed names, final links/special files, parent-link limits and injected partial/sync failures. The initial Unix socket fixture exceeded the platform path-length limit; it was moved to a short disposable directory, then passed. A reviewer's sandbox denied socket binding with `EPERM`; root's authorized local-socket validation passed without skipping the test.

### M1a pure model-assistance core: implementation and local evidence

The accepted October 6 model-assistance scope now has a pure `rangoon-model-assistance` crate. The frozen [M1a contract](model-context-core.md) defines `prepare_pack` and `validate_response`, closed input and response JSON, canonical UTF-8 serialization, task/template binding, exact byte ranges and domain-separated pack identities. It preserves every selected byte or rejects the pack; required protected intervals must remain covered by protected selections. Exact whole-block deduplication retains per-input aliases and scope, and omissions remain explicit. Counts measure bytes, not token savings or semantic equivalence. The immutable serializable pack and response records have private fields and no deserialization constructor.

The preflight decoder checks raw size and UTF-8, nesting, values, object/array counts, decoded key/string lengths, field and aggregate ceilings before typed decoding. It scans value strings without allocating a complete decoded copy, rejects decoded duplicate keys, invalid escapes/surrogates, noninteger or oversized numeric tokens, and trailing JSON. Response validation binds task/kind and exact selected source/revision citations to the local immutable pack. Returned authored text remains inert and every validated response reports `authority: none`; valid citations do not prove an assertion true.

The primary controller owns the contract, decoder, adversarial integration tests and validation. OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra high produced the packer, response validator, task templates and core tests. A separate OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra high worker constructed six independent Python vectors from the specification and templates without reading the Rust implementation. They cover all tasks, exact BOM/CRLF/Unicode bytes, protected/overlapping/disjoint selections, identical text under distinct references, changed revisions and inert hostile text. The fixture SHA-256 is `67feb457366cee4b79ddf6af6a6a00bc393cc9e9b2435a904f486c8b73fa0851`; the Rust integration test matches canonical body, pack and validated-response bytes/digests. Exact raw response JSON is retained in the fixture so its hash is not changed by object-key reserialization.

Fresh OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra xhigh contract review required explicit required-protection obligations and fixed task/template binding; both were resolved before acceptance of the implementation contract. Independent decoder review required numeric-width limit classification and explicit BOM/CRLF and total-value boundary evidence; the corrections passed final re-review. Separate final core review found one P3 test gap for declared `maxBodyBytes` and `maxOutputTokens` limits; the additional test accepts both exact maxima and rejects zero and one-over values. Final core re-review passed with no open P1/P2/P3 findings. The reviewers inspected exact files and did not rerun the controller's test suite. OpenAI GPT-5.6-Luna medium updated the README and architecture, with controller integration. No GLM invocation or external-provider source transfer occurred.

Local validation passed `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, warnings-denied workspace/all-target Clippy and `cargo test --workspace --locked --offline`: 184 tests passed, with five intentionally ignored subprocess helper entries exercised by parent tests. The first sandbox run failed the two existing CLI one-second child-process deadlines with `engine command read stdin`; the authorized rerun passed without changes to CLI behavior. After the final review added one boundary test, the complete model-assistance crate passed all 21 tests (nine decoder, seven adversarial, four core and one integration test covering six vectors). `python3 scripts/generate-model-context-fixtures.py --check` passed. The final test adds one unique check beyond the full workspace run; no second complete workspace run is claimed here. No registry dependency version changed: the root lockfile adds only the new local crate and its existing workspace dependencies.

This packet does not connect native/CLI/UI code, open files/databases, send HTTP, access credentials, install a model, apply a proposal or activate LNSAT. The pure packer accepts caller-resolved content and cannot establish database existence, ancestry, consent or peer identity. Its provider-neutral body is not the final HTTP request. M1b–M1e must qualify native resolution, final payload/configuration binding, explicit operator decisions, bounded local/cloud adapters, secret custody, UI/application and model quality. Security certification, government compliance, token-compression quality and V1 completion remain unclaimed. ### M1a first hosted Windows failure and correction

The first PR21 head `9411c9110805a3fa54db646dd5fd09965d2cf0eb` failed Windows source tests in push run `37442111929` and PR run `37442167934`. Six adversarial checks returned `InputInvalid` or failed their success assertion because Git's Windows checkout converted the compiled `.txt` templates to CRLF; the exact `Task: <task>` plus LF check correctly rejected those changed bytes. macOS and Linux source checks passed at that head. The controller reproduced the conversion with `git -c core.autocrlf=true cat-file --filters HEAD:<template>`: the first template had six CRLF sequences and no exact LF header.

A targeted `.gitattributes` rule now pins only the model-assistance template directory's text files to LF. It does not normalize imported sources or weaken template/pack identity checks. ## M1b local transport foundation — October 6, 2026

Authority remains the accepted October 6 continuation in [intent.md](intent.md). The new [local adapter contract](model-local-adapter.md) freezes immutable numeric-loopback profiles, canonical final HTTP request binding, a strict Ollama-compatible subset, bounded response decoding, monotonic cancellation and a process-wide one-flight guard. Fresh OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra xhigh contract review required exact origin bytes, a restricted model-label grammar, explicit u16 port bounds, a process-wide rather than per-client limit and exact Content-Type parameter syntax. Those corrections were applied; contract re-review passed.

`rangoon-model-local` implements explicit source-free `GET /api/version` and prepared `POST /api/chat` over direct Tokio TCP and Hyper HTTP/1 to `127.0.0.1` or `::1`. No resolver, environment proxy, pool, redirect following, retry, credential or model-launch path exists. Limits are 3 seconds to connect, 5 seconds for a check, 120 seconds for analysis, 256 KiB final request, 1 KiB version response, 1 MiB chat response and 128 KiB decoded proposal content. Every operation owns its connection-driving future; cancellation, timeout and future drop close it and release the shared guard. Responses cannot create authority or alter request identity. Loopback peer identity, processing location and retention remain unverified.

Sol integrated the profile, request and transport implementation. OpenAI GPT-5.6-Terra high workers supplied the bounded wire decoder, independent Python byte/hash oracle and synthetic HTTP fixtures; OpenAI GPT-5.6-Luna medium updated the README and architecture. Exact immutable getters were added to the M1a pack without changing its serialized bytes or six golden vectors. Twenty registry packages were added for the direct HTTP client; no existing locked registry version changed. No GLM lane was invoked: bounded native GPT lanes were available, and no source was transferred to a separate model provider.

Development checks caught and corrected a repeated UTF-8 suffix scan in the response preflight, a malformed test match arm, and incomplete HTTP bodies being classified as invalid rather than incomplete. The decoder now scans Unicode bytes linearly after one UTF-8 validation pass. Initial sandbox socket tests failed with `Operation not permitted`; explicitly authorized disposable-loopback runs passed outside that sandbox. No real model daemon, private input or credentials were used. Initial build errors for SHA-256 formatting and an unavailable client-only Hyper error helper were corrected before the successful checks.

Local validation on Homebrew Rust/Cargo 1.98.0 passed the full `cargo test --workspace --locked` suite: 205 tests, zero failures, five intentionally ignored subprocess helpers exercised by their parent tests. The local-adapter subset passed 20 tests (nine unit, five identity, six HTTP integration). ### PR24 hosted test-handshake correction

Initial reviewed head `0f855a78c9df724ab1547b8697d080d97531d501` was held from merge. In PR run `37499670793`, Linux source job `112393129521` failed the existing `interrupted_public_recovery_preserves_schema_rows_reviews_and_heads` test at `composition_recovery_tests.rs:84`: the checkpoint file existed but its contents were still empty rather than `after_delete_rows`. The child used `fs::write` while the parent treated file existence as a complete ready signal; file creation precedes the data write. This failure is in the test-process handshake, not an observed recovery assertion or new model path.

The test-only fault checkpoint now writes a sibling pending marker and renames the complete file into place. ## Native cloud preparation: authenticated checks and operation ownership

This in-progress continuation refines the accepted October 6 model/security intent in [model-cloud-native.md](model-cloud-native.md). It deliberately separates the portable authenticated check and native ownership fixes from the remaining native cloud orchestration. The desktop still cannot invoke a cloud check or send. No cloud command, review window, credential-use conversion, or route selector has been enabled by this portion.

The cloud library adds immutable `CheckRequest` identity and `CloudClient::check`: fixed-origin authenticated GET for the explicitly configured model, empty body, 30-second total deadline, shared 10-second connection deadline, 65,536-byte response cap, and the existing TLS/address/proxy/redirect/retry restrictions. Strict bounded metadata validation requires the requested model identity and a valid optional calendar shutdown date. Receipts report model visibility only and unknown inference compatibility, processing location, retention and cost. The portable session now reserves and validates a `Resolution` before a native OS credential read; cancelled/cleared resolutions cannot subsequently bind a revision. It also exposes a source-free check operation lease without doing any I/O.

The native local controller now uses an application-wide `ModelFlight` gate for configure/prepare/check/send. Pending consent retains its actual transmission and gate ownership if the IPC waiter disappears. An outer drop guard cancels retained workspace work, and freshness callbacks retain the shared gate until their reads finish. The blocking OS-dialog callback owns its decision and cleanup, including negative cleanup during unwinding; it no longer depends on the awaiting confirm IPC handler surviving. Clear/cancel remain available while operations are busy. Future cloud orchestration must use this same gate and the existing separate credential-custody slot.

Initial independent contract review found that full-envelope comparison was stated for analysis only, even though a check also transfers credentials. The revised contract requires the identical first-read/retained-envelope/consent/final-full-envelope-comparison lifecycle for both. ### PR28 first-head CI diagnosis

The reviewed twenty-path source was committed as `7409836c1d2cbedf3a4cb5d04656fba5d08ff79c` and pushed in [PR #28](https://github.com/hypler-dev/rangoon/pull/28). Push run `37523589785` failed Windows Rust job `112474860019` in the pre-existing CLI engine test: its one-second open-stdin watchdog emitted `engine command read stdin`. The log establishes deadline expiry, not an actual stdin read; inspection confirms that the inert engine branch returns before the analyzer's stdin reader. macOS/Linux Rust checks passed for the same head. No merge was attempted with the failed check.

The bounded follow-up changes only `crates/rangoon-cli/tests/cli.rs` plus this evidence: retain the open stdin pipe and forced child cleanup, allow ten seconds for hosted process startup/scheduling, and report watchdog expiry without asserting an unobserved stdin read. This changes no production model or engine deadline and enables no engine operation. The original failure remains in `/private/tmp/rangoon-cloud-native-ownership-windows-failure.log`. Focused pinned CLI validation, independent review and a new exact-head hosted run are required before publication. The complete PR now contains twenty-one paths.

Focused Rust 1.85 CLI formatting, seven tests (six CLI plus one golden fixture) and warnings-denied all-target Clippy passed after the watchdog adjustment. Fresh independent GPT-5.6-Terra-xhigh review passed the exact test/evidence follow-up with no P1/P2/P3 findings. The PR-triggered Windows Rust job also passed the original head independently; the failed push job remains a failure rather than being erased. This supports treating the one-second deadline as unreliable test infrastructure, without asserting a measured startup cause.

## Native cloud dispatch and workbench continuation

Authority remains the accepted October 6 model-assistance continuation in [intent.md](intent.md), refined by [model-cloud-native.md](model-cloud-native.md). On branch `codex/model-cloud-dispatch`, based on PR28 merge `920368f418ac15cada46b3f90e4dc2cea4dfaf64`, the desktop now implements the native cloud path rather than leaving the portable adapter disconnected. Publication remains pending at this entry.

Seven main commands configure, inspect, clear, prepare, check, send and cancel a session. Three separately permissioned child commands expose the retained request, request a final OS decision, or cancel. Cloud profiles fix the origin; page load and route changes do not inspect credentials or call a provider. The cloud workbench accepts only native DTOs and renders selected-source coverage, exact body, body/request hashes, credential revision, provider-declared check metadata and explicit unknowns. Model output remains inert advisory text with no apply, review, approval, execution or engine authority.

Preparation retains its portable operation lease until the exact request and full zeroizing credential envelope are paired under the short native lock. Analysis and source-free checking hold both the shared local/cloud model gate and the credential-use slot through blocking callbacks, consent and completion. A second OS read compares the entire retained envelope, including same-revision changed key bytes. Missing, changed, malformed or unavailable credentials fail closed. Cancellation, clear, IPC drop and window close cannot revive stale work or release ownership while a callback still runs. Analysis checks saved-input freshness before and after transport. These are in-process and point-in-time checks, not an interprocess lock, authenticated SQLite integrity or protection against a compromised user session.

#### Direct composition canvas validation

The implemented graph edits the existing ordered recipes in Decompose, Merge and Split. Source sections have named text ports; copy/replacement/authored pieces preserve their exact ranges, text and explanations. Operators can drag a piece, select its port and a destination with pointer or keyboard, open visible Actions or right-click/Shift+F10 menus, add an output or authored block, and undo/redo local changes. The controller retains at most 32 history entries and 4 MiB of serialized combined undo/redo state. History includes only recipe and destination state. Stale captured versions, external files and cancelled drag payloads cannot mutate the draft. Pending native operations lock edits. Undo/redo cannot restore acknowledgments, preview authority or save receipts. Source ports accept only wholly unassigned sections; replacement, exclusion and deliberate duplication continue through explicit source actions.

Review found and corrected focus restoration/menu dismissal, cancelled-drag custody, stale click-to-connect versions, positional form-cache remapping during source-range edits and external draft replacement, stale committed fields after external single-field updates, and hidden port endpoints. The form cache now retains only user edits relative to the prior model-rendered baseline, captured before restoring edits; successful structural transitions clear positional state. Unsafe UI structural edits require Apply/Cancel first. Failed actions preserve fields. Wire drawing caches source ranges and port geometry once per draw, avoiding repeated section-map construction inside the piece/section loop. This is bounded source reasoning, not a performance benchmark.

## E1a optional keyed SQLite backend — October 7, 2026

Authority: Jeff asked to address database encryption under the dated encryption steering in [intent.md](intent.md). The [encrypted workspace contract](encrypted-workspace.md) freezes E1a ownership and acceptance. Initial inspection confirmed native SQLite and all portable backups were plaintext; API OS credential custody and unkeyed digests did not change that boundary. M1d-2 retained proposal inspection was discovered read-only and remains the next application slice after the storage decision; no model/UI/native source changed here.

Implemented `rangoon-store/encrypted-sqlite` as an optional pinned `rusqlite =0.40.2` / `libsqlite3-sys =0.38.2` bundled Community SQLCipher 4.14.0 backend, with existing exact `zeroize =1.9.0`. Public `WorkspaceKey` has neither Debug nor serialization, and explicit `Workspace::encrypted` verifies an in-memory keyed backend/profile before disk access. Default builds return `EncryptionUnavailable`, with no filesystem creation or downgrade. Legacy `Workspace::new` remains plaintext. The key remains host-owned, zeroizing on final shared drop; no entropy generation, OS key lookup, renderer key, migration or erasure-of-all-copies claim is included.

Keyed file connections verify the pinned profile/provider label, disable SQLCipher logging, restrict temporary storage to memory, reject misaligned or pre-existing zero-length files, authenticate main-file pages without exposing diagnostic-row text and verify readable schema before existing transactions/record validation. Only a zero file exclusively created by the current write can initialize. Wrong key, plaintext and keyed-content rejection share the closed `encrypted_store_invalid` code. Existing directories/no-follow/bounds/schema/identity/journal behavior remains. The full-page authentication scan is per connection, bounded by the 64 MiB store ceiling; no performance or post-check/rollback guarantee is inferred.

The first clean-file synthetic tests exposed SQLCipher's text-valued `page_size` getter (`InvalidColumnType(0, "cipher_page_size", Text)`), rather than a SQLite integer. Ownership was explicitly extended to getter-only substitutions in the five affected inventory/recovery/workflow files before editing. A shared canonical decimal/integer parser preserves existing bounds and schema/record semantics. An early module test incorrectly expected rekeying an existing test connection to retain a changed local profile; the corrected isolated subprocess changes process defaults before constructor verification, which must reject before disk access, and the separate connection test verifies mismatching settings are rejected without rewriting them. A test-only borrow conflict and overly long import fixture line were fixed without weakening behavior assertions. Temporary synthetic diagnostics were removed before final gates.

### E1b-2a source qualification and directory-custody refinement

The native library implements the frozen exclusive owner without mounting it into production commands. `Owner` holds the cooperating control-file lease, strictly bounded nonsecret identity binding, exact prepared source/action and managed key/store session. Create saves the selected report into a separate encrypted identity directory; unlock authenticates an existing nonempty database before opening a session. Managed list/open/save revalidate custody and the complete vault record; lock drops managed key/store/pending state while retaining cooperating-owner custody. Missing keys never regenerate, and uncertain writes block further same-owner actions until explicit fresh inspection. Existing plaintext commands, source selection, compilation, composition, workflows, models, exports and portable backups stay unchanged.

Independent fixtures found and closed an actual directory-custody gap: a replaced canonical identity directory could reach vault lookup before eventual database authentication failed. The separately reviewed contract refinement now retains parent and identity-directory proofs through prepared unlock and opened sessions. Unix proofs hold no-follow, directory-only descriptors and dev/inode metadata, preventing inode reuse while retained; compare them before vault access and after actual storage operations. Prepared unlock with a copied valid database under a replacement directory also refuses before vault lookup. Non-Unix metadata/canonical/non-reparse checks remain narrower; no Windows inode-identity or runtime claim follows. The cooperating control lease, source semantics, public APIs and dependency versions stay unchanged.

### E1b-2b1 implementation and validation receipt

Pre-code independent contract review returned PASS after two clarifications. Retained Model flight admission coexists with source/read lanes, but existing compound local/cloud model prepare and freshness inspections retain their short Exclusive lease to exclude in-process source mutations. Downgrading those inspections to concurrent reads would weaken the existing freshness contract. Admission helpers now distinguish capacity Busy from every closed, stale, poisoned or exhausted state; only actual capacity contention keeps existing busy responses. Other failures map to existing unavailable/session-unavailable diagnostics. The expanded ownership below was frozen before code changes.

The native library adds an opaque shared Gate with 64 counted concurrent Read leases, one existing source/picker Exclusive lease and one independent Model lease. The Builder manages one gate and gives ModelFlight a clone of the same instance. Existing picker, mutation, composition, workflow, backup/restore/deletion, compilation, export/inspection and compound model callbacks retain their actual worker leases; formerly unguarded snapshot/capability/workflow/data reads now acquire counted Read before dispatch and retain it inside their blocking callbacks. Drain atomically closes admission and checked-increments generation; old leases become noncurrent while their lane remains held until final owner drop. A gate-bound token reports quiescence only after every lane becomes idle. Exact idle resume increments generation again; stale/wrong tokens refuse, dropped tokens never reopen, overflow permanently closes and poison stays unavailable. No gate mutex spans IO, a dialog or an await. No transition command is registered. Callback quiescence is not renderer delivery fencing, data purge, a locked database, rollback or execution authority.

Sixteen independent synthetic gate fixtures cover reader capacity/recovery, lane coexistence, all-owner drain, callback Arc custody, active/wrong/reused tokens, second-drain refusal, old-token invalidation across reopen, dropped-token closure, both transition-overflow paths, wrong-gate precedence, sticky poison with retained owners, restricted test seeding, coordinated thread drain/admission and unwind release. Native helper/ModelFlight fixtures additionally cover exact busy/unavailable mapping and shared source/read/model callback custody. Source review traces actual Builder and endpoint wiring; these fixtures do not invoke real Tauri windows or prove GUI behavior. Eight new compile-fail privacy checks prevent clone/debug/serialization of custody owners (Gate itself intentionally remains clonable).

Technical outcome: root and an independent read-only Terra-xhigh native reviewer examined existing ready-state setup faults and volatile post-save recovery. The contract separates committed data from result delivery, defines a bounded failure/reconciliation matrix, preserves clean successful restart, and rejects a sole deleted marker, Complete-before-Ready ordering and a permanent Pending fence as complete usable solutions. Final-write observation cannot establish caller acknowledgment after arbitrary storage failure. No source mechanism is selected or claimed implemented. A terminal-state/reconciliation protocol must pass pre-code review before protected storage edits; this unresolved decision remains the next engineering gate. Existing empty initialization, routing and close/purge foundations are reused.

Experience outcome: no controls, assets, motion, routes or layouts change. Preserve the original compact workbench and node-editor direction. Future recovery should provide a short explicit inspection/confirmation path rather than expose storage internals or make ordinary clean restart permanently unusable.

Security/standards impact: nonsecret recovery metadata still carries identity and outcome meaning. The proposed controls must qualify exact identities, filesystem custody, no vault access on refused states, no automatic retry or destructive repair, plus OS-specific durability. This is architectural evidence supporting the existing ISO/OpenSSF readiness track; it establishes no certification, maturity level, deployed encryption, authenticated anti-rollback or hardware-failure guarantee.
